### Conclusion

- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: Quantum Communication
- **Published**: 2026-06-03
- **Authors**: Kai-Chi Chang, Xiang Cheng, Yujie Chen And Chee Wei Wong
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6f45](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6f45)
- **Tags**: `tin`  `entanglement`  `quantum frequency combs`  `Hong-Ou-Mandel interference`  `photon bunching`

### TL;DR

First experimental observation of asymmetric Hong-Ou-Mandel revival interference using quantum frequency combs, demonstrating photon bunching recurrences over multiple time-bins and distribution over 21 km fiber.

### Motivation

To systematically investigate asymmetric HOM revival interference in QFCs, which had not been experimentally demonstrated, and to show efficient distribution of photon-bunching revivals for quantum communication applications.

### Method

Experimental setup with 10.21 GHz and 50 GHz QFCs, measuring HOM interference visibilities over multiple time-bins, and distributing the QFC over 21 km optical fiber without dispersion compensation.

### Result

Observed HOM revivals spanning up to 20 time-bins with central dip visibilities around 70-73%, and distributed QFC over 21 km fiber with 12 time-bins and 70.5% visibility. Deviation from ideal HOM interference attributed to residual frequency distinguishability.

### Conclusion

QFCs are a viable platform for advanced quantum information processing, including high-dimensional entanglement swapping and quantum communication testbeds.

*Keywords: quantum frequency combs, Hong-Ou-Mandel interference, photon bunching, quantum communication, entanglement distribution*

## 79. Exponential Lindbladian Fast Forwarding And Exponential Amplification Of Certain Gibbs State Properties

- **Score**: 7.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-05-27
- **Authors**: Dong An And Changpeng Shao Zhong-Xia Shang
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae6df7](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae6df7)
- **Tags**: `tin`  `complexity`  `Lindbladian fast-forwarding`  `Gibbs state`  `quantum algorithm`

### TL;DR

This paper presents quantum algorithms for exponential fast-forwarding of Lindbladian evolution and uses them to estimate Gibbs state properties with exponential improvement in circuit depth under certain coherence conditions.

### Motivation

Fast-forwarding of Hamiltonian dynamics is well-studied, but analogous results for dissipative Lindbladian dynamics are lacking. The authors aim to fill this gap and apply fast-forwarding to efficiently estimate Gibbs state properties, which is crucial for quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics.

### Method

The authors develop a quantum algorithm for simulating purely dissipative Lindbladians with unitary jump operators, achieving additive query complexity. For structured jump operators (block-diagonal Paulis), they modify the algorithm to achieve exponential fast-forwarding with circuit depth O(log(1/ε)) while preserving query complexity via parallel access. They then use these techniques to estimate Gibbs state properties of the form Tr[O ρ_β] with exponential improvement in circuit depth when the input state satisfies certain coherence conditions.

### Result

The algorithm achieves exponential fast-forwarding for Lindbladian evolution with structured jump operators, and provides exponential improvement in circuit depth for estimating Gibbs state properties under coherence conditions. Applications to ground state overlap testing and amplitude estimation are shown.

### Conclusion

This work opens new avenues for efficient simulation of dissipative quantum dynamics and estimation of thermal properties, with potential impact on quantum thermodynamics and quantum information processing. Future work may explore broader classes of Lindbladians and relax coherence requirements.

*Keywords: Lindbladian fast-forwarding, Gibbs state, quantum algorithm, dissipative dynamics, circuit depth*

### TL;DR

The paper shows that the quantum Zeno effect, induced by environmental decoherence, severely limits the speedup of adiabatic quantum algorithms like Grover's search, and suggests that similar limitations apply to quantum annealing schemes.

### Motivation

To understand how decoherence affects the performance of adiabatic quantum computing and quantum annealing, particularly in the context of the quantum Zeno effect.

### Method

The authors analyze the adiabatic version of Grover's algorithm under a general coupling to an environment, using the quantum Zeno effect as a framework to study decoherence-induced measurement-like effects.

### Result

They find that the environment effectively measures the system, inhibiting transitions and thus limiting the quantum speedup; this is generalized to Landau-Zener type transitions in adiabatic algorithms and quantum annealing.

### Conclusion

The results imply that gradual state changes (second-order phase transitions) or error correction (e.g., spin echo) may mitigate the problem, but the quantum Zeno effect poses fundamental limitations.

### Method

The authors derive a schedule that keeps the geometric speed constant, defined as the rate of change of the adiabatic parameter weighted by the metric on the parameter manifold. They analyze the scaling of the evolution time with the path length and energy gap.

### Result

The constant geometric speed schedule achieves the rigorous lower bound on evolution time, scaling as O(L^2/Δ^2) where L is the adiabatic path length and Δ is the minimum gap, improving over traditional schedules.

### Conclusion

This work provides an optimal scheduling strategy for adiabatic state preparation, which can reduce resource requirements in quantum computing and simulation. Future work may extend to non-adiabatic or open systems.

*Keywords: adiabatic state preparation, geometric speed, quantum evolution, energy gap, optimal schedule*

### TL;DR

This paper establishes fundamental lower bounds on the number of non-Clifford gates required to generate pseudorandom unitaries, showing that extensive doping is necessary.

### Motivation

Understanding the resource cost of generating pseudorandom quantum states and unitaries is crucial for quantum cryptography and quantum advantage experiments. The Clifford group is classically simulable, so non-Clifford gates are needed for randomness; quantifying this cost is a key open problem.

### Method

The authors use techniques from quantum information theory, including entropy accumulation and properties of stabilizer states, to derive lower bounds on the number of T gates (or other non-Clifford gates) needed to achieve approximate unitary t-designs.

### Result

They prove that to generate a unitary that is ε-close to Haar random in the sense of a t-design, the number of non-Clifford gates must scale at least linearly with the number of qubits n, and for constant t, the required number is Ω(n). This shows that extensive doping is necessary.

### Conclusion

The results provide fundamental limits on the efficiency of pseudorandom unitary generation, with implications for quantum cryptography, quantum advantage, and the design of random circuits. Future work may explore tighter bounds or connections to other resource theories.

*Keywords: non-Clifford gates, pseudorandom unitaries, unitary t-designs, Clifford circuits, quantum resource theory*

### TL;DR

This paper proposes an efficient predecision scheme to reduce computational complexity of Metropolis Monte Carlo simulations for long-range interacting lattice systems.

### Motivation

Simulating long-range interacting lattice systems with Metropolis Monte Carlo is computationally expensive due to O(N^2) interactions per step. The authors aim to reduce this complexity to O(N) for certain potentials.

### Method

The predecision scheme uses a precomputed table of acceptance probabilities based on energy differences, allowing fast rejection or acceptance of moves without full energy calculation.

### Result

For potentials decaying as 1/r^{d+σ}, the computational complexity is reduced from O(N^2) to O(N) for σ > d, and to O(N log N) for σ = d.

### Conclusion

The scheme enables efficient simulation of large long-range interacting systems, with potential applications in statistical physics and materials science.

*Keywords: Metropolis Monte Carlo, long-range interactions, computational complexity, lattice systems, predecision scheme*

### TL;DR

This paper presents a hybrid quantum-classical variational optimization pipeline for binary combinatorial problems on gate-model quantum computers, achieving high approximation ratios on up to 156 qubits.

### Motivation

To demonstrate that gate-model quantum computers can solve NP-hard binary optimization problems at scale with an integrated error-suppressed pipeline, overcoming the limitations of naive implementations.

### Method

The method combines a custom variational ansatz, staged feedback-based dual variational parameter updates, efficient parametric compilation, automated error suppression, and scalable classical post-processing for bitflip error correction.

### Result

The pipeline achieves 100% approximation ratios for Max-Cut on unweighted 3-regular graphs up to 156 nodes and at least 99.5% for spin-glass models up to 156 qubits, outperforming classical local solvers.

### Conclusion

The integrated approach enables gate-model quantum computers to produce high-quality solutions for binary optimization problems at the 156-qubit scale, suggesting that variational methods with error suppression are viable for near-term quantum advantage.

## 268. Geometry-resolved Suppression Of Patch-potential Heating In Skeleton-electrode Ion Traps

- **Score**: 2.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-01
- **Authors**: Chon-Teng Belmiro Chu, Hao-Chung Chen, Ting Hsu, Hsiang-Yu Lo, Ming-Shien Chang And Guin-Dar Lin
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6e99](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae6e99)
- **Tags**: `tin`  `three-dimensional`  `confinement`  `ion trap`  `patch potential`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a geometry-resolved framework to evaluate patch-potential heating in ion traps, demonstrating that skeleton-electrode designs can suppress motional heating by over 50% compared to conventional blade traps.

### Motivation

Motional heating from patch potentials is a major decoherence source in trapped-ion quantum computing. The authors aimed to understand how electrode geometry influences heating and to design traps with reduced noise.

### Method

They developed a quantitative framework to compute patch-potential noise in realistic 3D Paul traps, applied it to a skeleton-electrode architecture and a conventional blade trap, and generated patch-by-patch heating maps to identify noise hotspots.

### Result

The skeleton geometry suppresses heating by >50% for both axial and radial modes. Over 99% of noise comes from electrodes within 500 μm of the ion, and axial heating has a non-monotonic profile with hotspots at ~110 μm. Reconfiguring electrode gaps to align with hotspots reduces heating even with increased surface area.

### Conclusion

Geometry-guided design is a practical route to suppress heating in ion traps, and the skeleton architecture is compatible with advanced fabrication like metal additive manufacturing.

*Keywords: ion trap, patch potential, motional heating, skeleton electrode, geometry-resolved*

---

### Motivation

To understand how the spatial arrangement of active units affects the collective mechanical response of active solids, particularly when active units are sparse.

### Method

Combined experiments with robotic metamaterials and theoretical modeling to study active solids with non-percolating active units.

### Result

When active units are too sparse to percolate, increasing their activity reduces the overall macroscopic response, a 'more is less' effect.

### Conclusion

The percolation of active forces is crucial for macroscopic activity; this work highlights the role of network topology in active matter.

*Keywords: active solids, percolation, metamaterials, active matter, robotic metamaterials*

---

- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-05-17
- **Authors**: Kexin Zhang And Zhengbao Yang
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae672b](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae672b)
- **Tags**: `tin`  `confinement`  `nanoparticles`  `crystallization`  `nucleation`

### TL;DR

This paper investigates electric-field-driven nanoconfinement to stabilize piezoelectric β-glycine nanocrystals within a 5–120 nm size range.

### Motivation

Controlled preparation of piezoelectric β-glycine is challenging; existing nanoconfinement methods cannot reliably capture the intrinsic confinement regime for β-phase formation.

### Method

Glycine nanoparticles were produced via electrohydrodynamic spraying under a DC field, with automatic variation of spraying height to modulate nanoconfinement during nucleation and growth.

### Result

Pure β-glycine forms within a crystal radius range of 5–120 nm; a crystallization phase map delineating confinement conditions for β-phase stabilization was constructed.

### Conclusion

This work identifies critical nanoconfinement parameters for accessing piezoelectric β-glycine and provides insights into polymorph control in molecular crystals.

*Keywords: β-glycine, nanoconfinement, piezoelectric, electrohydrodynamic spraying, polymorph control*

- **Authors**: Jinwoo Kim, Ingyu Yoo, Jaewoong Joo, Hyoungseok Lee, Jundong Yeo, Jian-Min Zuo, Miyoung Kim And Gwan-Hyoung Lee
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae5ea2](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae5ea2)
- **Tags**: `tin`  `strain`  `compression`  `twist`  `microscopy`

### TL;DR

This paper studies strain relaxation in MoS2/WS2 and MoS2/WSe2 hetero-bilayers using STEM and moiré pattern analysis, focusing on lattice mismatch effects in van der Waals epitaxy.

### Motivation

To understand how lattice mismatch influences strain relaxation and interface structure in van der Waals heterostructures, which is important for epitaxial growth and device quality.

### Method

Epitaxial growth of MoS2 on WS2 or WSe2 substrates, followed by annular dark-field scanning transmission electron microscopy (ADF-STEM) and moiré pattern analysis.

### Result

MoS2/WS2 (0.22% mismatch) forms a fully commensurate structure, while MoS2/WSe2 (3.96% mismatch) shows incommensurate moiré patterns with strain-driven lattice rotation and elongation.

### Conclusion

Moiré pattern analysis is a powerful tool for visualizing strain relaxation in 2D heterostructures, revealing that large mismatch favors lattice rotation over commensurate alignment.

*Keywords: strain relaxation, moiré pattern, van der Waals epitaxy, transition metal dichalcogenides, STEM*

- **Tags**: `prediction`  `cross-section`  `top quark pair production`  `non-relativistic QCD`  `quasi-bound states`

### TL;DR

This paper reports an observation of a cross-section enhancement near the top quark pair production threshold in proton-proton collisions, consistent with quasi-bound state formation in non-relativistic QCD.

### Motivation

To investigate the production of top quark pairs near threshold, where non-perturbative effects from quasi-bound states may become significant, and to test predictions of non-relativistic QCD.

### Method

The ATLAS detector at the LHC collected data at 13 TeV, and a profile-likelihood fit was performed on reconstructed distributions in bins of two angular observables sensitive to spin correlations.

### Result

An excess of events over the baseline pQCD prediction was observed with a significance over 8 standard deviations, corresponding to a cross-section of 2.1 pb, consistent with colour-singlet and spin-singlet S-wave quasi-bound states.

### Conclusion

The results support the formation of quasi-bound states near threshold, providing a test of non-relativistic QCD and improving understanding of top quark pair production dynamics.

*Keywords: top quark pair production, non-relativistic QCD, quasi-bound states, ATLAS, cross-section enhancement*

To provide direct field observations of self-accelerating turbidity currents, which have been inferred from deposits and cable breaks but never directly measured.

The authors deployed an array of instruments in a submarine canyon to measure flow velocity, sediment concentration, and seafloor erosion during a turbidity current event.

### Result

They observed a self-accelerating flow where erosion increased sediment concentration, which in turn increased flow velocity, leading to further erosion.

### Conclusion

These findings confirm the existence of self-accelerating turbidity currents and highlight their role in shaping submarine landscapes and damaging infrastructure.

### TL;DR

This paper studies the influence of oxidative stress and serum deprivation on the evolution of newly formed tetraploid cells during tumorigenesis, a topic in cell biology and cancer research.

### Motivation

To understand the mechanisms linking whole genome doubling, extra centrosomes, and tumorigenesis, which are important for tumor progression.

### Method

Experimental cell biology approaches, likely involving cell culture under stress conditions and analysis of tetraploid cell behavior.

### Result

Oxidative stress and serum deprivation affect the evolution of newly formed tetraploid cells, potentially promoting tumorigenesis.

### Conclusion

The findings provide insights into how environmental stressors influence genomic instability and cancer development, with implications for cancer therapy.

*Keywords: whole genome doubling, tetraploid cells, oxidative stress, tumorigenesis, centrosomes*

- **Tags**: `polyploidy`  `dilation`  `Shroom3`  `hyperpolyploidy`  `ventricular dilation`

### TL;DR

This paper identifies Shroom3 as a driver of hyperpolyploidy and ventricular dilation in cardiomyocytes through genome-wide association mapping and loss-of-function studies.

### Motivation

To understand the genetic mechanisms governing cardiomyocyte ploidy, which varies across species and is linked to heart disease.

### Method

Genome-wide association mapping in rats and targeted loss-of-function experiments in mice and zebrafish.

### Result

Shroom3 is identified as a key regulator of cardiomyocyte polyploidy and ventricular dilation.

### Conclusion

Shroom3 may be a therapeutic target for heart conditions involving ploidy changes.

*Keywords: Shroom3, hyperpolyploidy, ventricular dilation, cardiomyocyte, genome-wide association*
